timestamps (~60 Hz) are separate from ophys timestamps (~11 Hz mesoscope, ~31 Hz single-plane)\n- Running speed has both raw and 10 Hz lowpass Butterworth filtered versions\n- Eye tracking has blink detection via z-score threshold (3.0) with 2-frame dilation\n- `dff_traces` stored as (timepoints x ROIs) in NWB, transposed to (ROIs x timepoints) on read\n- Getting dF/F array: `np.vstack(dataset.dff_traces.dff.values)` → shape (n_cells, n_timepoints)\n\n### Data Flow\n```\nNWB File → BehaviorOphysExperiment.from_nwb(nwbfile)\n  ├── ophys_timestamps (array, seconds)\n  ├── dff_traces (DataFrame: cell_specimen_id → dff array)\n  ├── events (DataFrame: events, filtered_events)\n  ├── cell_specimen_table (ROI metadata, valid_roi filter)\n  ├── running_speed (DataFrame: timestamps, speed in cm/s)\n  ├── eye_tracking (DataFrame: timestamps, pupil_area, etc.; NaN for blinks)\n  ├── licks (DataFrame: timestamps)\n  ├── rewards (DataFrame: timestamps, volume, auto_rewarded)\n  ├── stimulus_presentations (DataFrame: image_name, start_time, is_change, omitted, etc.)\n  └── trials (DataFrame: go, catch, hit, miss, false_alarm, correct_reject, aborted, auto_rewarded, etc.)\n```\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\n- 284 NWB files in `behavior_ophys_experiments/` directory\n- 4 metadata CSV files in 5 µL | Whitepaper |\n\n### Processing Details\n- **dF/F**: Pre-computed in NWB. Algorithm: noise estimation → 600s median filter baseline → (F-F0)/F0 → detrending\n- **Calcium events**: FastLZeroSpikeInference with L0 regularization. Multiplicative factor: 2.0 (31 Hz) or 2.6 (11 Hz)\n- **filtered_events**: Events convolved with causal half-Gaussian (scale 2.0/31.0 s)\n- **Running speed**: 10 Hz lowpass Butterworth filter, z-score ≥ 10 transients set to NaN\n- **Pupil**: DeepLabCut tracking, ellipse fit, blinks detected by z-score > 3, dilated 2 frames, set to NaN\n- **Temporal alignment**: All data streams synchronized via NI PCI-6612 at 100 kHz\n- **Paper neural analysis window**: 50-800 ms after image onset (interpolated to 30 Hz)\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules** (from SDK/whitepaper):\n- `valid_roi` filter: excludes unions of cells, duplicates (>70% overlap), edge ROIs, apical dendrites, too small/narrow/dim, ghost cells (crosstalk), negative/zero traces\n- Classification via linear SVC on features: depth, shape, area, intensity, SNR, etc.\n\n**Session-level QC** (from whitepaper):\n- Z-drift < 10 µm\n- Baseline fluorescence drop < 20%\n- Pixel saturation < 1000 pixels\n- d-prime ≥ 1.0\n- No excessive residual motion\n- No interictal events\n- Data synchronization OK\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n- Per task instructions: Include Go and Catch trials, exclude Aborted and Auto-rewarded\n- Paper excludes images where licking bout already ongoing\n\n### Decoders Trained (from Piet et al.)\n| Decoded variable | Method | Accuracy |\n|---|---|---|\n| Change vs repeat | Random Forest, 5-fold CV, first 400ms | 55-75% |\n| Hit vs miss | Random Forest, 5-fold CV, first 400ms | 55-80% |\n| False alarm | Random Forest | Very low |\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for blink detection z>3 | Use area → compute diameter, interpolate NaNs |\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------|-------|\n| dF/F traces | neural | Average within 750ms stimulus bins | dff_traces in NWB | (n_neurons, n_timepoints) per trial |\n| (none) | input | Empty array | N/A | No inputs per task spec |\n| image_name | output[0] | Categorical encoding (8 images) | stimulus_presentations | Forward-fill for omitted |\n| is_change | output[1] | Binary (0/1) | stimulus_presentations | 1 at change flash only |\n| running speed | output[2] | Average in 750ms bins, discretize to 5 percentile bins | running/speed in NWB | 10 Hz Butterworth filtered |\n| pupil area → diameter | output[3] | 2*sqrt(area/pi), interpolate NaN, avg in bins, 5 percentile bins | pupil_tracking/area | Interpolate blinks |\n| hit/miss/fa/cr | output[4] | Categorical (4 classes) | trials table | Static per trial |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Time bin = 750ms (1 per stimulus flash)**: Natural task unit, consistent across all equipment types (MESO/CAM2P), aligned to image presentations. Each bin = 250ms image + 500ms grey.\n2. **Use dF/F (not events)**: dF/F is the standard neural signal for decoding; pre-computed in NWB; events are sparser and may not decoder as well for time-varying outputs.\n3. **Active sessions only**: Passive sessions have no meaningful trial outcomes (no licking).\n4. **valid_roi filter**: Use only cells marked valid_roi=True in NWB, matching SDK default behavior.\n5. **Omitted stimuli**: Forward-fill image identity from previous presentation. Neural and behavioral data still recorded during omissions.\n6. **Pupil NaN handling**: Linear interpolation for blink frames before computing diameter and averaging.\n7. **Discretization**: Compute percentile bin edges across ALL valid time points in dataset (2-pass), then assign bins.\n8. **Brain regions**: From targeted_structure in experiment metadata (VISp, VISl in our subset).\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] Trial count per session matches trials table (Go+Catch, non-aborted, non-auto-rewarded)\n- [ ] Number of stimulus presentations per trial matches NWB\n- [ ] dF/F values are reasonable (not all zeros, similar range across sessions)\n- [ ] Image identity distribution: 8 images roughly uniform\n- [ ] Change fraction: ~1 change per trial (~87.5% Go trials)\n- [ ] Running speed range matches expected cm/s values\n- [ ] Pupil diameter range is reasonable\n- [ ] Trial outcome distribution: mostly Misses + CRs (from example session: 307 misses, 16 hits)\n- [ ] Number of neurons per session matches valid_roi count from NWB\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\nImplementation: `convert_data.py` with two-pass approach:\n1. Pass 1: Collect running speed and pupil statistics for percentile bin computation\n2. Pass 2: Full conversion with discretization using global percentile bins\n\nOptimizations:\n- Cumulative sum-based bin averaging (avoids per-bin boolean masking)\n- np.searchsorted for efficient bin boundary finding\n- Sample 10 experiments for image name collection (not all 202)\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Sample Statistics (2 sessions)\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Sessions | 2 |\n| Subjects | 2 |\n| Trials (total) | 227 |\n| Trials/session | 39, 188 |\n| Neurons/session | 89, 27 |\n| Time bins/trial (mean) | 11.2 |\n| Image identity range | [0, 7] |\n| Image change distribution | 92.4% no_change, 7.6% change |\n| Running speed bins | 20% each (uniform) |\n| Pupil diameter bins | 20% each (uniform global) |\n| Trial outcomes | 40.1% hit, 47.1% miss, 4.0% FA, 8.8% CR |\n\n### Run Time Estimates\n| Step | Time (2 sessions) | Estimated (202 sessions) |\n|------|-------------------|--------------------------|\n| Pass 1 | 5.7s | ~575s |\n| Pass 2 | 8.7s | ~878s |\n| Total | 14.6s | ~1453s (~24min) |\n\n---\n\n## Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Format Validation\n- Errors: None\n- Warnings: None\n\n### Decoder Results (Sample, 2 sessions, 181 train / 46 test)\n| Output | Training Balanced Acc | Validation Balanced Acc | Chance |\n|--------|----------------------|------------------------|--------|\n| image_identity | 0.2412 | 0.1715 | 0.1250 |\n| image_change | 0.6887 | 0.6667 | 0.5000 |\n| running_speed | 0.2952 | 0.3109 | 0.2000 |\n| pupil_diameter | 0.2801 | 0.2445 | 0.2000 |\n| trial_outcome | 0.3830 | 0.2689 | 0.2500 |\n\nAll outputs above chance on training data. The neural data is derived from the **dF/F traces** stored in `processing/ophys/dff/traces/data` in the NWB files. This is the pre-computed delta-F-over-F calcium fluorescence signal.\n\nii.\n```python\ndff_data = f['processing']['ophys']['dff']['traces']['data'][:]  # (timepoints, neurons)\nophys_ts = f['processing']['ophys']['dff']['traces']['timestamps'][:]\n```\n\niii. The AI documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 1 that \"dF/F is pre-computed in NWB files\" and chose dF/F over calcium events because \"dF/F is the standard neural signal for decoding; pre-computed in NWB; events are sparser.\" The AI acknowledged that the paper (Piet et al.) uses calcium events for its neural analysis but decided dF/F was more appropriate for the decoding task.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. The neural data is averaged within 750ms bins corresponding to each stimulus presentation. For each stimulus presentation, the code finds ophys frames that fall within [start_time, start_time + 0.75s) and averages the dF/F values across those frames. This is done efficiently using cumulative sums and searchsorted.\n\nii.\n```python\nbin_duration = TIME_BIN_MS / 1000.0  # 0.75s\nophys_bin_starts = np.searchsorted(ophys_ts, all_stim_starts, side='left')\nophys_bin_ends = np.searchsorted(ophys_ts, all_stim_ends, side='left')\ndff_cumsum = np.cumsum(dff_valid, axis=0)\ndff_cumsum = np.vstack([np.zeros((1, n_neurons), dtype=dff_cumsum.dtype), dff_cumsum])\n# ...\nfor bi, si in enumerate(trial_stim_indices):\n    s, e = ophys_bin_starts[si], ophys_bin_ends[si]\n    n_frames = e - s\n    if n_frames > 0:\n        neural_matrix[:, bi] = (dff_cumsum[e] - dff_cumsum[s]) / n_frames\n```\n\niii. The AI decided on 750ms bins (one per stimulus presentation: 250ms image + 500ms grey screen) as the natural task unit that is consistent across both equipment types (MESO at ~11 Hz and CAM2P at ~31 Hz).\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Only neurons/ROIs marked as `valid_roi=True` in the NWB cell specimen table are included. Experiments with 0 valid neurons are skipped.\n\nii.\n```python\ncell_table = f['processing']['ophys']['image_segmentation']['cell_specimen_table']\nvalid_roi = cell_table['valid_roi'][:].astype(bool)\ndff_valid = dff_data[:, valid_roi]\nn_neurons = dff_valid.shape[1]\nif n_neurons == 0:\n    print(f\"  WARNING: Experiment {experiment_id} has 0 valid neurons, skipping\")\n    return None\n```\n\niii. The two-pass approach ensures percentile bins are computed globally across all data, not per-session.\n\n## 5-c. How is `output` *Running speed* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Running speed is discretized into 5 equal percentile bins (quintiles). The bin edges are computed from all running speed values across all sessions, with the first edge set to -inf and the last to +inf.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize_values(values, n_bins, bin_edges=None):\n    if bin_edges is None:\n        valid = values[~np.isnan(values)]\n        percentiles = np.linspace(0, 100, n_bins + 1)\n        bin_edges = np.percentile(valid, percentiles)\n        bin_edges[0] = -np.inf\n        bin_edges[-1] = np.inf\n    binned = np.digitize(values, bin_edges[1:-1]).astype(np.int64)\n    binned = np.clip(binned, 0, n_bins - 1)\n    return binned, bin_edges\n```\n\niii. The instruction says \"discretized into five equal percentile bins.\" The AI computes global percentile edges: [-inf, 0.004, 0.788, 15.82, 33.13, inf] cm/s.\n\n## 5-d. Additionally, trials with no associated stimulus presentations are skipped.\n\nii.\n```python\ntrial_mask = (trial_go | trial_catch) & ~trial_aborted & ~trial_auto\n# ...\nif result is None or result['n_trials'] < 2:\n    skipped += 1\n    ...\n    continue\n# ...\ntrial_stim_indices = np.where(stim_trials_id == tid)[0]\nif len(trial_stim_indices) == 0:\n    continue\n```\n\niii. The AI documented that the instruction says to include Go and Catch trials, excluding Aborted and Auto-rewarded. The 2-trial minimum comes from the target format requirement (\"at least two trials within each session\").\n\n## 2-a. What variables in the raw data is the `neural` data derived from?\n\ni. The neural data is derived from the **dF/F traces** stored in `processing/ophys/dff/traces/data` in the NWB files. This is the pre-computed delta-F-over-F calcium fluorescence signal.\n\nii.\n```python\ndff_data = f['processing']['ophys']['dff']['traces']['data'][:]  # (timepoints, neurons)\nophys_ts = f['processing']['ophys']['dff']['traces']['timestamps'][:]\n```\n\niii. The AI documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.md Step 1 that \"dF/F is pre-computed in NWB files\" and chose dF/F over calcium events because \"dF/F is the standard neural signal for decoding; pre-computed in NWB; events are sparser.\" The AI acknowledged that the paper (Piet et al.) uses calcium events for its neural analysis but decided dF/F was more appropriate for the decoding task.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. The neural data is averaged within 750ms bins corresponding to each stimulus presentation. For each stimulus presentation, the code finds ophys frames that fall within [start_time, start_time + 0.75s) and averages the dF/F values across those frames. This is done efficiently using cumulative sums and searchsorted.\n\nii.\n```python\nbin_duration = TIME_BIN_MS / 1000.0  # 0.75s\nophys_bin_starts = np.searchsorted(ophys_ts, all_stim_starts, side='left')\nophys_bin_ends = np.searchsorted(ophys_ts, all_stim_ends, side='left')\ndff_cumsum = np.cumsum(dff_valid, axis=0)\ndff_cumsum = np.vstack([np.zeros((1, n_neurons), dtype=dff_cumsum.dtype), dff_cumsum])\n# ...\nfor bi, si in enumerate(trial_stim_indices):\n    s, e = ophys_bin_starts[si], ophys_bin_ends[si]\n    n_frames = e - s\n    if n_frames > 0:\n        neural_matrix[:, bi] = (dff_cumsum[e] - dff_cumsum[s]) / n_frames\n```\n\niii. The AI decided on 750ms bins (one per stimulus presentation: 250ms image + 500ms grey screen) as the natural task unit that is consistent across both equipment types (MESO at ~11 Hz and CAM2P at ~31 Hz).\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Only neurons/ROIs marked as `valid_roi=True` in the NWB cell specimen table are included. Experiments with 0 valid neurons are skipped.\n\nii.\n```python\ncell_table = f['processing']['ophys']['image_segmentation']['cell_specimen_table']\nvalid_roi = cell_table['valid_roi'][:].astype(bool)\ndff_valid = dff_data[:, valid_roi]\nn_neurons = dff_valid.shape[1]\nif n_neurons == 0:\n    print(f\"  WARNING: Experiment {experiment_id} has 0 valid neurons, skipping\")\n    return None\n```\n\niii. The AI noted that this matches the SDK's default behavior (`exclude_invalid_rois=True` in `CellSpecimens`), which filters unions, duplicates, edge ROIs, apical dendrites, too small/narrow/dim ROIs, ghost cells, and negative/zero traces.\n\n## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n\ni. Neural data is aligned to stimulus presentation onsets. Each time bin corresponds to one stimulus presentation, starting at `start_time` and extending for 750ms. The ophys timestamps are used as the temporal reference, and frames falling within each 750ms window are averaged.\n\nii.\n```python\nall_stim_starts = stim_start  # from stimulus presentations table\nall_stim_ends = all_stim_starts + bin_duration  # start + 0.75s\nophys_bin_starts = np.searchsorted(ophys_ts, all_stim_starts, side='left')\nophys_bin_ends = np.searchsorted(ophys_ts, all_stim_ends, side='left')\n```\n\niii. The metadata records `temporal_alignment_event` as \"Stimulus presentation onset (each 750ms image flash).\"\n\n## 2-e. What is the temporal resolution (time bin size) of the converted data? Is any temporal rebinning applied?\n\ni. The time bin size is 750ms, corresponding to one stimulus presentation interval (250ms image + 500ms grey screen). This constitutes temporal rebinning from the native ophys frame rate (~11 Hz for mesoscope or ~31 Hz for single-plane) down to ~1.33 Hz (one bin per 750ms).\n\nii.\n```python\nTIME_BIN_MS = 750.0  # One bin per stimulus presentation (250ms image + 500ms grey)\n```\n\niii. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Image identity*?\n\ni. Image names are collected across experiments, sorted, and assigned integer indices. For omitted stimulus presentations (where `image_name` is 'omitted'), the AI forward-fills the image identity from the previous non-omitted presentation. Images are then encoded as integer category indices using a global mapping.\n\nii.\n```python\nimages = stim_image_name[trial_stim_indices].copy()\n# Forward-fill omitted presentations\nfor bi in range(len(images)):\n    if images[bi] == 'omitted':\n        if bi > 0:\n            images[bi] = images[bi - 1]\n        else:\n            for bj in range(bi + 1, len(images)):\n                if images[bj] != 'omitted':\n                    images[bi] = images[bj]\n                    break\nimage_indices = np.array([img_to_idx.get(img, 0) for img in images], dtype=np.int64)\n```\n\niii. The AI documented that 5% of non-change presentations are omitted, and forward-filling ensures continuous image identity labels. It collects all unique image names across experiments (16 total: 8 per image set A/B).\n\n## 3-c. How is `output` *Image identity* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Image identity is directly aligned with neural data because both use the same stimulus presentation bins. Running speed is averaged within each 750ms stimulus bin using cumulative sums and searchsorted for efficiency. Then, values are discretized into 5 equal percentile bins using global percentile edges computed across all sessions in Pass 1.\n\nii.\n```python\n# Bin averaging:\nrun_bin_starts = np.searchsorted(running_ts, all_stim_starts, side='left')\nrun_bin_ends = np.searchsorted(running_ts, all_stim_ends, side='left')\nrun_cumsum = np.cumsum(running_speed)\nrun_cumsum = np.concatenate([[0], run_cumsum])\n# ...\nfor bi, si in enumerate(trial_stim_indices):\n    s, e = run_bin_starts[si], run_bin_ends[si]\n    n_pts = e - s\n    if n_pts > 0:\n        running_binned[bi] = (run_cumsum[e] - run_cumsum[s]) / n_pts\n\n# Discretization (global percentile bins):\n_, running_bin_edges = discretize_values(all_running, N_PERCENTILE_BINS)\nrunning_disc, _ = discretize_values(ot['running_speed_raw'], N_PERCENTILE_BINS, running_bin_edges)\n```\n\niii. The two-pass approach ensures percentile bins are computed globally across all data, not per-session.\n\n## 5-c. How is `output` *Running speed* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Running speed is discretized into 5 equal percentile bins (quintiles). The bin edges are computed from all running speed values across all sessions, with the first edge set to -inf and the last to +inf.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize_values(values, n_bins, bin_edges=None):\n    if bin_edges is None:\n        valid = values[~np.isnan(values)]\n        percentiles = np.linspace(0, 100, n_bins + 1)\n        bin_edges = np.percentile(valid, percentiles)\n        bin_edges[0] = -np.inf\n        bin_edges[-1] = np.inf\n    binned = np.digitize(values, bin_edges[1:-1]).astype(np.int64)\n    binned = np.clip(binned, 0, n_bins - 1)\n    return binned, bin_edges\n```\n\niii. The instruction says \"discretized into five equal percentile bins.\" The AI computes global percentile edges: [-inf, 0.004, 0.788, 15.82, 33.13, inf] cm/s.\n\n## 5-d. Processing steps: (1) Set blink frames to NaN, (2) Set negative/zero values to NaN, (3) Convert area to diameter via `d = 2*sqrt(area/pi)`, (4) Linearly interpolate NaN values, (5) Average within 750ms bins, (6) Handle remaining NaNs with interpolation, (7) Discretize into 5 equal percentile bins using global edges.\n\nii.\n```python\npupil_area = pupil_area_raw.copy().astype(float)\npupil_area[likely_blink] = np.nan\npupil_area[pupil_area <= 0] = np.nan\npupil_diameter = 2.0 * np.sqrt(pupil_area / np.pi)\npupil_diameter = interpolate_nans(pupil_diameter)\n# ...\n# Bin averaging with NaN-aware cumsum\n# Discretization with global percentile bins\n```\n\niii. NaN-aware averaging is used (cumulative sum of valid values divided by count of valid values).\n\nii.\n```python\npup_bin_starts = np.searchsorted(pupil_ts, all_stim_starts, side='left')\npup_bin_ends = np.searchsorted(pupil_ts, all_stim_ends, side='left')\n# NaN-aware averaging\npup_valid = ~np.isnan(pupil_diameter)\npup_filled = np.where(pup_valid, pupil_diameter, 0.0)\npup_cumsum = np.cumsum(pup_filled)\n```\n\niii.
